The breach occurred through compromised credentials that allowed unauthorized access to the university's identity and access management system, affecting both current and former users.

 - DTU's identity management system was breached, affecting 200,000 users.
 - Exposed data could lead to identity fraud and phishing attempts.

Differences exist between how heart attacks present in men and women, with women often experiencing atypical symptoms that may be mistaken for less serious conditions. Cardiovascular disease risk factors, symptoms, and the critical timeframe for treatment are essential for improving outcomes in women. Due to various biological factors, including hormonal differences, women may face higher mortality rates from heart attacks if symptoms are not correctly identified and treated promptly.

 - Heart attack symptoms in women can differ significantly from those in men.
 - Women have specific cardiovascular risk factors that can lead to higher mortality rates.
 - Timely recognition and treatment of heart attack symptoms are crucial for better survival outcomes.

Source: https://www.mkpenza.ru/social/2026/10/04/penzenskiy-kardiolog-obyasnila-chem-zhenskiy-infarkt-otlichaetsya-ot-muzhskogo.html
